Chapter 8
Haar distributed process tensors

Here we present the derivation of the expressions presented in Chaper
4
for the expectation of quantum processes
\Upsilon
and the expectation of the purity, or noisiness, of quantum processes
\tr\left(\Upsilon^{2}\right)
, according to the Haar measure.

Appendix 8.A
Average processes - Constant interaction

We are considering here
\Upsilon
to be unnormalized (or more precisely, normalized to
\tr(\Upsilon)=d_{\mathsf{S}}^{k+1}
), as presented first in the main text in Eq. (
11
). Also, here
\Upsilon
stands for a
k
-step process
\Upsilon_{k:0}
as done in the main text as well.

By definition,

\displaystyle\mathds{E}_{\mathsf{h}}(\Upsilon)

\displaystyle=\tr_{\mathsf{E}}\sum_{\alpha,\ldots,\gamma=1}^{d_{\mathsf{S}}}\,\int_{\mathds{U}(d_{\mathsf{SE}})}U\FS_{\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}}\cdots{U}\FS_{\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}}U\,\rho\,{U}^{\dagger}\FS_{\gamma_{1}\delta_{1}}^{\dagger}{U}^{\dagger}\cdots\FS_{\gamma_{k}\delta_{k}}^{\dagger}{U}^{\dagger}\,d\mu_{\mathsf{h}}(U)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\otimes|\beta_{1}\alpha_{1}\ldots\beta_{k}\alpha_{k}\rangle\!\langle\delta_{1}\gamma_{1}\ldots\delta_{k}\gamma_{k}|,

(1)

where
\FS_{\alpha\beta}:=\mathds{1}_{\mathsf{E}}\otimes|\alpha\rangle\!\langle\beta|
. We now decompose the unitaries as
U=\sum{U}_{ab}|a\rangle\!\langle{b}|
and
U^{\dagger}=\sum{U}^{*}_{a^{\prime}{b}^{\prime}}|b^{\prime}\rangle\!\langle{a}^{\prime}|
–notice that the
a
and
b
labels refer to the whole
SE
space–, introducing a resolution of the identity on
E
within the
\FS
operators as
\FS_{ab}=\sum_{e}|ea\rangle\!\langle{eb}|
, and then evaluating the
k+1
moments of
\mathds{U}(d_{\mathsf{SE}})
by means of the moments of the unitary group in Eq. (
6
),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{U}(d_{\mathsf{SE}})}U\FS_{\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}}\cdots{U}\FS_{\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}}U\rho{U}^{\dagger}\FS_{\gamma_{1}\delta_{1}}^{\dagger}{U}^{\dagger}\cdots\FS_{\gamma_{k}\delta_{k}}^{\dagger}{U}^{\dagger}\,d\mu_{\mathsf{h}}(U)

\displaystyle=\sum\int_{\mathds{U}(d_{\mathsf{SE}})}U_{i_{0}j_{0}}\cdots{U}_{i_{k}j_{k}}U^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{0}j^{\prime}_{0}}\cdots{U}^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{k}j^{\prime}_{k}}\,d\mu_{\mathsf{h}}(U)\,|i_{k}\rangle\!\langle{j}_{0}|\rho|j^{\prime}_{0}\rangle\!\langle{i}^{\prime}_{k}|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\delta_{j_{\ell}(e\alpha)_{\ell}}\delta_{(e\beta)_{\ell}{i}_{\ell-1}}\delta_{j^{\prime}_{\ell}(e^{\prime}\gamma)_{\ell}}\delta_{(e^{\prime}\delta)_{\ell}{i^{\prime}}_{\ell-1}}

\displaystyle=\sum\sum_{\sigma,\tau\in\mathfrak{G}_{k+1}}\langle{j}_{0}|\rho|j^{\prime}_{0}\rangle\delta_{i_{0}i^{\prime}_{\sigma(0)}}\delta_{j_{0}j^{\prime}_{\tau(0)}}\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\delta_{i_{\ell}{i}^{\prime}_{\sigma(\ell)}}\delta_{j_{\ell}{j}^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell)}}\delta_{j_{\ell}(e\alpha)_{\ell}}\delta_{(e\beta)_{\ell}{i}_{\ell-1}}\delta_{j^{\prime}_{\ell}(e^{\prime}\gamma)_{\ell}}\delta_{(e^{\prime}\delta)_{\ell}{i^{\prime}}_{\ell-1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathsf{Wg}(\tau\sigma^{-1})\,|i_{k}\rangle\!\langle{i}^{\prime}_{k}|

\displaystyle=\sum\sum_{\sigma,\tau\in\mathfrak{G}_{k+1}}\langle{j}^{\prime}_{\tau(0)}|\rho|j^{\prime}_{0}\rangle\delta_{i_{k}i^{\prime}_{\sigma(k)}}\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\delta_{(e\beta)_{\ell}{i}^{\prime}_{\sigma(\ell-1)}}\delta_{(e^{\prime}\delta)_{\ell}{i^{\prime}}_{\ell-1}}\delta_{(e\alpha)_{\ell}{j}^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell)}}\delta_{(e^{\prime}\gamma)_{\ell}{j}^{\prime}_{\ell}}\,\mathsf{Wg}(\tau\sigma^{-1})\,|i_{k}\rangle\!\langle{i}^{\prime}_{k}|,

(2)

where here
\mathfrak{G}_{k+1}
denotes the symmetric group on
\{0,1,\ldots,k\}
,
\mathsf{Wg}
is the Weingarten function with implicit dimensional argument
d_{\mathsf{SE}}
, and which taking
i\to\epsilon\varsigma
and
j\to\epsilon^{\prime}\varsigma^{\prime}
to recover each
E
and
S
part explicitly, turns into

\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{U}(d_{\mathsf{SE}})}U\FS_{\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}}\cdots{U}\FS_{\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}}U\,\rho\,{U}^{\dagger}\FS_{\gamma_{1}\delta_{1}}^{\dagger}{U}^{\dagger}\cdots\FS_{\gamma_{k}\delta_{k}}^{\dagger}{U}^{\dagger}\,d\mu(U)

\displaystyle=\sum\sum_{\sigma,\tau\in\mathfrak{G}_{k+1}}\langle\epsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(0)}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(0)}|\rho|\epsilon^{\prime}_{0}\varsigma^{\prime}_{0}\rangle\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\delta_{\epsilon_{\sigma(\ell-1)}\epsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell)}}\delta_{\epsilon_{\ell-1}\epsilon^{\prime}_{\ell}}\delta_{\beta_{\ell}\varsigma_{\sigma(\ell-1)}}\delta_{\delta_{\ell}\varsigma_{\ell-1}}\delta_{\alpha_{\ell}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell)}}\delta_{\gamma_{\ell}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\ell}}\,\mathsf{Wg}(\tau\sigma^{-1})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad|\epsilon_{\sigma(k)}\varsigma_{\sigma(k)}\rangle\!\langle\epsilon_{k}\varsigma_{k}|,

(3)

and thus

\displaystyle\mathds{E}_{\mathsf{h}}(\Upsilon)=\sum\sum_{\sigma,\tau\in\mathfrak{G}_{k+1}}\hskip-7.22743pt\langle\epsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(0)}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(0)}|\rho|\epsilon^{\prime}_{0}\varsigma^{\prime}_{0}\rangle\,\mathsf{Wg}(\tau\sigma^{-1})\,\delta_{\epsilon_{\sigma(k)}\epsilon_{k}}\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\delta_{\epsilon_{\sigma(\ell-1)}\epsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell)}}\delta_{\epsilon_{\ell-1}\epsilon^{\prime}_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad|\varsigma_{\sigma(k)}\varsigma_{\sigma(0)}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(1)}\cdots\varsigma_{\sigma(k-1)}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(k)}\rangle\!\langle\varsigma_{k}\varsigma_{0}\varsigma^{\prime}_{1}\cdots\varsigma_{k-1}\varsigma^{\prime}_{k}|,

(4)

as stated by Eq.(
11
).

1
Superchannel case

For the superchannel case,
k=1
, we have
\mathfrak{G}_{2}=\{(0,1),(0)(1)\}
where the elements are permutations stated in
cycle
notation, representing the assignments
(0,1):0\to 1\to 0
and
(0)(1)=\boldsymbol{1}^{2}=0\to 0;1\to 1
, then (we write
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta
for
\varsigma_{0},\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{0}^{\prime},\varsigma_{1}^{\prime}
, respectively to ease the notation)

\displaystyle\mathds{E}_{\mathsf{h}}(\Upsilon_{1:0})=\frac{\sum}{d_{\mathsf{S}}}\bigg\{

\displaystyle\left[\langle\delta|\rho_{\mathsf{S}}|\gamma\rangle|\alpha\beta\gamma\rangle\!\langle\beta\alpha\delta|+d_{\mathsf{E}}^{2}|\beta\alpha\delta\rangle\!\langle\beta\alpha\delta|\right]\mathsf{Wg}[\boldsymbol{1}^{2}]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+d_{\mathsf{E}}\left[\langle\delta|\rho_{\mathsf{S}}|\gamma\rangle|\beta\alpha\gamma\rangle\!\langle\beta\alpha\delta|+|\alpha\beta\delta\rangle\!\langle\beta\alpha\delta|\right]\mathsf{Wg}[(0,1)]\bigg\},

(5)

so that with
\mathsf{Wg}[\boldsymbol{1}^{2},d]=\frac{1}{d^{2}-1}
and
\mathsf{Wg}[(0,1),d]=\frac{-1}{d(d^{2}-1)}

[
187
]
we get

\displaystyle\mathds{E}_{\mathsf{h}}(\Upsilon_{1:0})=\frac{1}{d_{\mathsf{E}}^{2}d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}-1}\bigg[\frac{d_{\mathsf{E}}^{2}}{d_{\mathsf{S}}}\mathds{1}_{\mathsf{SAB}}+\frac{\swap}{d_{\mathsf{S}}}\otimes\rho_{\mathsf{S}}^{\mathrm{T}}-\frac{\swap}{d_{\mathsf{S}}}\otimes\frac{\mathds{1}_{\mathsf{B}}}{d_{\mathsf{S}}}-\frac{\mathds{1}_{\mathsf{SA}}}{d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}\otimes\rho_{\mathsf{S}}^{\mathrm{T}}\bigg],

(6)

where
\swap=\sum_{i,j}|ij\rangle\!\langle{j}i|
is the usual swap operator, and hence for the corresponding purity one may verify that

\displaystyle\tr[\mathds{E}_{\mathsf{h}}(\Upsilon_{1:0})^{2}]=\frac{2}{(d_{\mathsf{E}}^{2}d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}-1)^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{d_{\mathsf{S}}^{3}}+\tr(\rho_{S}^{2})\frac{d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}-d_{\mathsf{S}}-1}{2d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}-\frac{d_{\mathsf{E}}^{2}}{d_{\mathsf{S}}}+\frac{d_{\mathsf{E}}^{4}d_{\mathsf{S}}}{2}\right].

(7)

Appendix 8.B
Average purity - Random interaction

Here
\Upsilon
is normalized to unit trace, in accordance with the main text. Let
\Theta=\rho\otimes\Psi^{\otimes{k}}
, where we assume
\rho
is pure,
\tr\rho=1
, and
\mathsf{U}_{k:0}:=U_{k}\mathcal{S}_{k}\cdots{U}_{1}\mathcal{S}_{1}U_{0}
, we first (following the approach in section 2 of
[
240
]
) write the trace as

\displaystyle\tr\left(\Upsilon^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\tr\left[\left(\tr_{\mathsf{E}}(\mathsf{U}_{k:0}\Theta\,\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}_{k:0})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\tr\left[\tr_{\mathsf{E}}\left((\mathds{1}_{E}\otimes\Upsilon)\,\mathsf{U}_{k:0}\Theta\,\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}_{k:0}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\tr\left[\Gamma_{\mathsf{E}}(\mathsf{U}_{k:0}\Theta\,\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}_{k:0})\,\mathsf{U}_{k:0}\Theta\,\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}_{k:0}\right]

\displaystyle=\tr\left[\Theta\,\mathsf{U}_{k:0}^{\dagger}\Gamma_{\mathsf{E}}(\mathsf{U}_{k:0}\Theta\,\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}_{k:0})\,\mathsf{U}_{k:0}\right],

(8)

where
\Gamma_{\mathsf{E}}(\cdot)\equiv\mathds{1}_{E}\otimes\tr_{\mathsf{E}}(\cdot)
is a CP map and can be expressed as an operator-sum, i.e. for any
\mathcal{X}
acting on
ESA_{1}B_{1}\cdots{A}_{k}B_{k}
we have
\Gamma_{\mathsf{E}}(\mathcal{X})=\sum_{i,j=1}^{d_{\mathsf{E}}}(K_{ij}\otimes\mathds{1})\mathcal{X}(K_{ij}\otimes\mathds{1})^{\dagger}
where the identities are on the ancillary system and
K_{ij}=|i\rangle\!\langle{j}|\otimes\mathds{1}_{S}
are the (
ES
system) Kraus operators with
\{|i\rangle\}_{i=1}^{d_{\mathsf{E}}}
a given basis for
E
. And so we need to compute the integrals

\displaystyle\Omega_{k:0}:=\int_{\mathds{U}(d_{\mathsf{SE}})}\mathsf{U}_{k:0}^{\dagger}\Gamma_{\mathsf{E}}(\mathsf{U}_{k:0}\Theta\,\mathcal{U}^{\dagger}_{k:0})\,\mathsf{U}_{k:0}\,d\mu_{\mathsf{h}}(U_{0})\cdots\,d\mu_{\mathsf{h}}(U_{k}),

(9)

and in fact these are really in the
SE
part only, which has the form

\displaystyle\omega_{k:0}^{(\rho)}:=\int_{\mathds{U}(d_{\mathsf{SE}})}

\displaystyle U_{0}^{\dagger}\FS_{x_{1}\alpha_{1}}U_{1}^{\dagger}\cdots{\FS}_{x_{k}\alpha_{k}}U_{k}^{\dagger}{K}_{\tilde{\imath}\tilde{\jmath}}U_{k}\FS_{\beta_{k}x_{k}}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdots{U_{1}}\FS_{\beta_{1}x_{1}}U_{0}\rho{U}_{0}^{\dagger}\FS_{y_{1}\lambda_{1}}U_{1}^{\dagger}\cdots\FS_{y_{k}\lambda_{k}}U_{k}^{\dagger}{K}^{\dagger}_{\tilde{\imath}\tilde{\jmath}}{U}_{k}\FS_{\theta_{k}y_{k}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdots{U}_{1}\FS_{\theta_{1}y_{1}}U_{0}\,d\mu_{\mathsf{h}}(U_{0})\,d\mu_{\mathsf{h}}(U_{1})\cdots d\mu_{\mathsf{h}}(U)_{k},

(10)

with summation over repeated indices
x,y
and
i,j
on the Kraus operators implied, and the ancillary part takes the form

\displaystyle\Omega_{k:0}^{(\mathsf{AB})}

\displaystyle:=(|\alpha_{1}\rangle\!\langle\beta_{1}|\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathsf{B}_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes|\alpha_{k}\rangle\!\langle\beta_{k}|\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathsf{B}_{k}})\mathsf{\Psi}^{\otimes\,k}(|\lambda_{1}\rangle\!\langle\theta_{1}|\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathsf{B}_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes|\lambda_{k}\rangle\!\langle\theta_{k}|\otimes\mathds{1}_{\mathsf{B}_{k}}).

(11)

We may then evaluate each of the integrals using

\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{U}(d)}U^{\dagger}{A}UX{U}^{\dagger}{B}U\,d\mu_{\mathsf{h}}(U)=\frac{d\tr(AB)-\tr(A)\tr(B)}{d(d^{2}-1)}\tr(X)\mathds{1}+\frac{d\tr(A)\tr(B)-\tr(AB)}{d(d^{2}-1)}X,

(12)

which can be seen to follow from the second moment of the unitary group (an explcit derivation can be seen e.g. in Ref.
[
194
]
), here with
d=d_{\mathsf{SE}}
, which from now on we employ, then we have

\displaystyle\omega_{k:0}^{(\rho)}=\frac{1}{d(d^{2}-1)}

\displaystyle\bigg\{\delta_{x_{1}y_{1}}\int_{\mathds{U}(d)}\tr\bigg[\FS_{\theta_{1}\alpha_{1}}U_{1}^{\dagger}\cdots{\FS}_{x_{k}\alpha_{k}}U_{k}^{\dagger}{K}_{ij}U_{k}\FS_{\beta_{k}x_{k}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdots{U}_{1}\FS_{\beta_{1}\lambda_{1}}U_{1}^{\dagger}\cdots\FS_{y_{k}\lambda_{k}}U_{k}^{\dagger}{K}^{\dagger}_{ij}{U}_{k}\FS_{\theta_{k}y_{k}}\cdots{U}_{1}\bigg](d\mathds{1}-\rho)\,d\mu_{\mathsf{h}}(U_{1})\cdots{d}\mu_{\mathsf{h}}(U_{k})

\displaystyle+\delta_{x_{1}x_{1}}\delta_{y_{1}y_{1}}\int\tr\bigg[\FS_{\beta_{1}\alpha_{1}}U_{1}^{\dagger}\cdots{\FS}_{x_{k}\alpha_{k}}U_{k}^{\dagger}{K}_{ij}U_{k}\FS_{\beta_{k}x_{k}}\cdots{U}_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\tr\bigg[\FS_{\theta_{1}\lambda_{1}}U_{1}^{\dagger}\cdots\FS_{y_{k}\lambda_{k}}U_{k}^{\dagger}{K}^{\dagger}_{ij}{U}_{k}\FS_{\theta_{k}y_{k}}\cdots{U}_{1}\bigg](d\rho-\mathds{1})\,\,d\mu_{\mathsf{h}}(U_{1})\cdots{d}\mu_{\mathsf{h}}(U_{k})\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d(d^{2}-1)}\bigg[d_{\mathsf{S}}\langle{e}_{1}\alpha_{1}|\omega_{k:1}^{(\FS_{\beta_{1}\lambda_{1}})}|e_{1}\theta_{1}\rangle(d\mathds{1}-\rho)+d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}\langle{e}_{1}\alpha_{1}|\omega_{k:1}^{(|e_{1}\beta_{1}\rangle\!\langle{e}^{\prime}_{1}\lambda_{1}|)}|e^{\prime}_{1}\theta_{1}\rangle(d\rho-\mathds{1})\bigg],

(13)

again with sum implied over
e_{i}
’s and Greek indices. Now, let us notice that

\displaystyle\omega_{k:i-1}^{(X)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d(d^{2}-1)}\bigg[d_{\mathsf{S}}\langle{e}_{i}\alpha_{i}|\omega_{k:i}^{(\FS_{\beta_{i}\lambda_{i}})}|e_{i}\theta_{i}\rangle(d\tr(X)\mathds{1}-X)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}\langle{e}_{i}\alpha_{i}|\omega_{k:i}^{(|e_{i}\beta_{i}\rangle\!\langle{e}^{\prime}_{i}\lambda_{i}|)}|e^{\prime}_{i}\theta_{i}\rangle(dX-\tr(X)\mathds{1})\bigg],

(14)

for
k>i-1\geq 0
. Then we get

\displaystyle\omega_{k:0}^{(\rho)}=\frac{1}{d^{2}(d^{2}-1)^{2}}

\displaystyle\bigg\{d_{\mathsf{S}}\langle{e}_{2}\alpha_{2}|\omega_{k:2}^{(\FS_{\beta_{2}\lambda_{2}})}|e_{2}\theta_{2}\rangle\bigg[d_{\mathsf{S}}(dd_{\mathsf{E}}^{2}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\theta_{1}}\delta_{\beta_{1}\lambda_{1}}-d_{\mathsf{E}}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}}\delta_{\lambda_{1}\theta_{1}})(d\mathds{1}-\rho)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}(dd_{\mathsf{E}}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\theta_{1}}\delta_{\beta_{1}\lambda_{1}}-d_{\mathsf{E}}^{2}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}}\delta_{\lambda_{1}\theta_{1}})(d\rho-\mathds{1})\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}\langle{e}_{2}\alpha_{2}|\omega_{k:2}^{(|e_{2}\beta_{2}\rangle\!\langle{e}_{2}^{\prime}\lambda_{2}|)}|e_{2}^{\prime}\theta_{2}\rangle\bigg[d_{\mathsf{S}}(dd_{\mathsf{E}}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}}\delta_{\lambda_{1}\theta_{1}}-d_{\mathsf{E}}^{2}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\theta_{1}}\delta_{\beta_{1}\lambda_{1}})(d\mathds{1}-\rho)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}(dd_{\mathsf{E}}^{2}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}}\delta_{\lambda_{1}\theta_{1}}\hskip-3.61371pt-d_{\mathsf{E}}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\theta_{1}}\delta_{\beta_{1}\lambda_{1}})(d\rho-\mathds{1})\bigg]\bigg\}.

(15)

Before carrying on, let us notice that the case
\omega_{k:k}
is special because here one evaluates the terms on the Kraus operators
\tr(K_{ij}K_{ij}^{\dagger})
and
\tr(K_{ij})\tr(K_{ij}^{\dagger})
summed over their indices, which leaves

\displaystyle\langle{e}_{k}\alpha_{k}|\omega_{k:k}^{(\FS_{\lambda_{k}\beta_{k}})}|e_{k}\theta_{k}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d(d^{2}-1)}\bigg[dd_{\mathsf{E}}^{2}(dd_{\mathsf{E}}\delta_{\alpha_{k}\theta_{k}}\delta_{\beta_{k}\lambda_{k}}-\delta_{\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}}\delta_{\lambda_{k}\theta_{k}})

(16)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+d^{2}d_{\mathsf{E}}(d_{\mathsf{S}}\delta_{\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}}\delta_{\lambda_{k}\theta_{k}}-\delta_{\alpha_{k}\theta_{k}}\delta_{\beta_{k}\lambda_{k}})\bigg],

(17)

and

\displaystyle\langle{e}_{k}\alpha_{k}|\omega_{k:k}^{(|e_{k}\beta_{k}\rangle\!\langle{e}^{\prime}_{k}\lambda_{k}|)}|e^{\prime}_{k}\theta_{k}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d(d^{2}-1)}\bigg[dd_{\mathsf{E}}^{3}(d_{\mathsf{S}}\delta_{\alpha_{k}\theta_{k}}\delta_{\beta_{k}\lambda_{k}}-\delta_{\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}}\delta_{\lambda_{k}\theta_{k}})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+d^{2}(dd_{\mathsf{E}}\delta_{\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}}\delta_{\lambda_{k}\theta_{k}}-\delta_{\alpha_{k}\theta_{k}}\delta_{\beta_{k}\lambda_{k}})\bigg].

(18)

Notice that we can get rid of the Kronecker deltas easily in the full
\Omega_{k:0}
when summing over Greek indices, as
\delta_{\alpha\theta}\delta_{\beta\lambda}
give rise to maximally mixed states in the ancillas while terms
\delta_{\alpha\beta}\delta_{\lambda\theta}
give rise to identities, and there are only deltas of this kind. Thus we may simply assign
\delta_{\alpha\theta}\delta_{\beta\lambda}\to 1/d_{\mathsf{S}}
and
\delta_{\alpha\beta}\delta_{\lambda\theta}\to 1
when plugging the corresponding expressions in
\Omega_{k:0}
.
1
1

1

In particular this implies that the terms
d_{\mathsf{S}}\delta_{\alpha\theta}\delta_{\beta\lambda}-\delta_{\alpha\beta}\delta_{\lambda\theta}
and
dd_{\mathsf{E}}\delta_{\alpha\beta}\delta_{\lambda\theta}-d_{\mathsf{E}}^{2}\delta_{\alpha\theta}\delta_{\beta\lambda}
won’t contribute to the average purity.
Furthermore, a direct consequence of this is that
\Omega_{k:0}
will be a linear combination of
2^{k+1}
tensor products between
\mathds{1}
,
\rho
and
\mathsf{\Psi}
, implying that (as
\rho
is pure,
\mathsf{\Psi}
is idempotent with trace one and the trace of an outer product is the product of traces) the average purity
\mathds{E}_{\mathfrak{h}}[\tr(\Upsilon^{2})]
will be a sum of the scalar terms in
\omega^{(\rho)}_{k:0}
together with an extra overall factor of
d-1
. Let us denote by

\displaystyle A=d_{\mathsf{E}}(d_{\mathsf{E}}^{2}-1),\quad B=d_{\mathsf{E}}^{2}-d_{\mathsf{E}}^{2}=0,\quad C=d_{\mathsf{E}}^{2}(d_{\mathsf{S}}-1/d_{\mathsf{S}}),\quad D=d_{\mathsf{E}}(dd_{\mathsf{E}}-1/d_{\mathsf{S}}),

(19)

the terms that appear in expression (
15
) after having taken
\delta_{\alpha\theta}\delta_{\beta\lambda}\to 1/d_{\mathsf{S}}
and

\delta_{\alpha\beta}\delta_{\lambda\theta}\to 1
. Doing similarly for the
\omega_{k:k}
case,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}=dd_{\mathsf{E}}^{2}(d_{\mathsf{E}}^{2}+d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2}-2),\quad\mathcal{B}=dd_{\mathsf{E}}(d^{2}-1),

(20)

we find that
2
2

2

A way to deduce this is to sub-label each factor
A,B,\cdots
by the
i
index of the term
{\langle{e}_{i}\alpha_{i}|\omega^{X_{i}}|e_{i}^{(\prime)}\theta_{i}\rangle}
that they come from and then substituting recursively in Eq. (
15
) as if one were already evaluating the whole purity. The vanishing of
B
simplifies this process a great deal.

\displaystyle\mathds{E}_{\mathfrak{h}}[\tr(\Upsilon^{2})]

\displaystyle=\frac{d_{\mathsf{S}}^{k}(d-1)}{[d(d^{2}-1)]^{k+1}}\left[\mathcal{A}\,A^{k-1}+d_{\mathsf{S}}\mathcal{B}\left(CA^{k-2}+C\sum_{i=1}^{k-2}d_{\mathsf{S}}^{i}D^{i}A^{k-i-2}+d_{\mathsf{S}}^{k-1}D^{k-1}\right)\right],

(21)

where the series has to be expanded before this can be evaluated; by means of the geometric series,
\sum_{i=1}^{n}x^{i}=\frac{x(x^{n}-1)}{x-1}
, i.e. with

\displaystyle A^{k-2}\sum_{i=1}^{k-2}\left(\frac{d_{\mathsf{S}}D}{A}\right)^{i}=Dd_{\mathsf{S}}\,\left(\frac{d_{\mathsf{S}}^{k-2}D^{k-2}-A^{k-2}}{d_{\mathsf{S}}D-A}\right),

(22)

then plugging in the dimensional values (with
d=d_{\mathsf{SE}}
) of all the constants we can simplify the purity to

\displaystyle\mathds{E}_{\mathfrak{h}}[\tr(\Upsilon^{2})]

\displaystyle=\frac{(d_{\mathsf{SE}}+1)\left(d_{\mathsf{SE}}^{2}-1\right)^{k}+\left(d_{\mathsf{E}}^{2}-1\right)^{k+1}}{d_{\mathsf{E}}(d_{\mathsf{SE}}+1)\left(d_{\mathsf{SE}}^{2}-1\right)^{k}},

(23)

which simplifies to the expression in Eq. (
27
).

Appendix 8.C
Average purity - Constant interaction

As in the previous Appendix
8.B
, here
\Upsilon
is normalized to unit trace. We may write the integral in the
ES
part, as in Eq.(
10
) now with same unitary (throughout we take sums over repeated indices),

\displaystyle\omega_{k:0}^{(\rho)}:=\int_{\mathds{U}(d_{\mathsf{SE}})}

\displaystyle U^{\dagger}\FS_{x_{1}\alpha_{1}}U^{\dagger}\cdots{\FS}_{x_{k}\alpha_{k}}U_{k}^{\dagger}{K}_{\tilde{\imath}\tilde{\jmath}}U\FS_{\beta_{k}x_{k}}\cdots{U}\FS_{\beta_{1}x_{1}}U\rho{U}^{\dagger}\FS_{y_{1}\lambda_{1}}U^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdots\FS_{y_{k}\lambda_{k}}U^{\dagger}{K}^{\dagger}_{\tilde{\imath}\tilde{\jmath}}{U}\FS_{\theta_{k}y_{k}}\cdots{U}\FS_{\theta_{1}y_{1}}U\,d\mu_{\mathsf{h}}(U),

(24)

We decompose the unitaries in the whole
SE
space as
U=\sum{U}_{ab}|a\rangle\!\langle{b}|
and
U^{\dagger}=\sum{U}^{*}_{a^{\prime}{b}^{\prime}}|b^{\prime}\rangle\!\langle{a}^{\prime}|
(i.e. the
a
,

labels here refer to the whole
SE

\rho
in Eq. (

) from

to

(priming,
′
, the adjoint ones) and the remaining from
k+1
to
2k+1
, we also do this in increasing order for the adjoint unitaries and decreasing order for the original unitaries so that they match the order of the
\FS
operators, i.e. the unitary components will originally appear as
U^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{0}j^{\prime}_{0}}\cdots{U}^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{k}j^{\prime}_{k}}U_{i_{k}j_{k}}\cdots{U_{i_{0}j_{0}}}U^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{k+1}j^{\prime}_{k+1}}\cdots{U}^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{2k+1}j^{\prime}_{2k+1}}U_{i^{\prime}_{2k+1}j^{\prime}_{2k+1}}\cdots{U_{i^{\prime}_{k+1}j^{\prime}_{k+1}}}
, and we then rearrange them to the form of Eq. (
6
) just keeping track of the correct order in the operator part, for which we introduce resolutions of identity on
E
into each
\FS

\FS_{ab}=\sum|ea\rangle\!\langle{eb}|
labeled by
e
for the ones to the left of
\rho
in Eq. (
24
), priming the ones between adjoint unitaries, and by
\epsilon
the corresponding ones to the right, also priming the ones between unitaries.

This then leads to

\displaystyle\omega_{k:0}^{(\rho)}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathds{U}(d_{\mathsf{SE}})}\hskip-7.22743ptU_{i_{0}j_{0}}\cdots{U}_{i_{2k+1}j_{2k+1}}U^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{0}j^{\prime}_{0}}\cdots{U}^{*}_{i^{\prime}_{2k+1}j^{\prime}_{2k+1}}\,d\mu_{\mathsf{h}}(U)\,\langle{i}^{\prime}_{k}|K_{\tilde{\imath}\tilde{\jmath}}|i_{k}\rangle\!\langle{j}_{0}|\rho|j^{\prime}_{k+1}\rangle\!\langle{i}^{\prime}_{2k+1}|K^{\dagger}_{\tilde{\imath}\tilde{\jmath}}|i_{2k+1}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\delta_{i_{\ell-1}(ex)_{\ell}}\delta_{j_{\ell}(e\beta)_{\ell}}\delta_{i_{\ell+k}(\epsilon{y})_{\ell}}\delta_{j_{\ell+k+1}(\epsilon\theta)_{\ell}}\delta_{i^{\prime}_{\ell-1}(e^{\prime}{x})_{\ell}}\delta_{j_{\ell}^{\prime}(e^{\prime}\alpha)_{\ell}}\delta_{i^{\prime}_{\ell+k}(\epsilon^{\prime}{y})_{\ell}}\delta_{j_{\ell+k+1}^{\prime}(\epsilon^{\prime}\lambda)_{\ell}}|j_{0}^{\prime}\rangle\!\langle{j}_{k+1}|

\displaystyle=\langle{i}^{\prime}_{k}|K_{\tilde{\imath}\tilde{\jmath}}|i_{k}\rangle\!\langle{j}_{0}|\rho|j^{\prime}_{k+1}\rangle\!\langle{i}^{\prime}_{2k+1}|K^{\dagger}_{\tilde{\imath}\tilde{\jmath}}|i_{2k+1}\rangle\sum_{\sigma,\tau\in\mathfrak{G}_{2k+2}}\!\!\mathsf{Wg}(\tau\sigma^{-1})\prod_{n=0}^{2k+1}\delta_{i_{n}i^{\prime}_{\sigma(n)}}\delta_{j_{n}j^{\prime}_{\tau(n)}}\,|j_{0}^{\prime}\rangle\!\langle{j}_{k+1}|

\displaystyle\qquad\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\delta_{i_{\ell-1}(ex)_{\ell}}\delta_{j_{\ell}(e\beta)_{\ell}}\delta_{i_{\ell+k}(\epsilon{y})_{\ell}}\delta_{j_{\ell+k+1}(\epsilon\theta)_{\ell}}\delta_{i^{\prime}_{\ell-1}(e^{\prime}{x})_{\ell}}\delta_{j_{\ell}^{\prime}(e^{\prime}\alpha)_{\ell}}\delta_{i^{\prime}_{\ell+k}(\epsilon^{\prime}{y})_{\ell}}\delta_{j_{\ell+k+1}^{\prime}(\epsilon^{\prime}\lambda)_{\ell}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma,\tau\in\mathfrak{G}_{2k+2}}\mathsf{Wg}(\tau\sigma^{-1})\langle{i}^{\prime}_{k}|K_{\tilde{\imath}\tilde{\jmath}}|i^{\prime}_{\sigma(k)}\rangle\!\langle{j}^{\prime}_{\tau(0)}|\rho|j^{\prime}_{k+1}\rangle\!\langle{i}^{\prime}_{2k+1}|K^{\dagger}_{\tilde{\imath}\tilde{\jmath}}|i^{\prime}_{\sigma(2k+1)}\rangle|j_{0}^{\prime}\rangle\!\langle{j}^{\prime}_{\tau(k+1)}|

\displaystyle\qquad\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\delta_{i^{\prime}_{\sigma(\ell-1)}(ex)_{\ell}}\delta_{j^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell)}(e\beta)_{\ell}}\delta_{i^{\prime}_{\sigma(\ell+k)}(\epsilon{y})_{\ell}}\delta_{j^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell+k+1)}(\epsilon\theta)_{\ell}}\delta_{i^{\prime}_{\ell-1}(e^{\prime}{x})_{\ell}}\delta_{j_{\ell}^{\prime}(e^{\prime}\alpha)_{\ell}}\delta_{i^{\prime}_{\ell+k}(\epsilon^{\prime}{y})_{\ell}}\delta_{j_{\ell+k+1}^{\prime}(\epsilon^{\prime}\lambda)_{\ell}}.

(25)

We now replace
i^{\prime}\to\varepsilon\varsigma
and
j^{\prime}\to\varepsilon^{\prime}\varsigma^{\prime}
in order to split the
E
and
S
parts explicitly, leaving

\displaystyle\omega_{k:0}^{(\rho)}

\displaystyle=\hskip-7.22743pt\sum_{\sigma,\tau\in\mathfrak{G}_{2k+2}}\hskip-10.84006pt\mathsf{Wg}(\tau\sigma^{-1})\langle\varepsilon_{k}\varsigma_{k}|K_{\tilde{\imath}\tilde{\jmath}}|\varepsilon_{\sigma(k)}\varsigma_{\sigma(k)}\rangle\!\langle\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(0)}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(0)}|\rho|\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k+1}\varsigma^{\prime}_{k+1}\rangle\!\langle\varepsilon_{2k+1}\varsigma_{2k+1}|K^{\dagger}_{\tilde{\imath}\tilde{\jmath}}|\varepsilon_{\sigma(2k+1)}\varsigma_{\sigma(2k+1)}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\delta_{(\varepsilon\varsigma)_{\sigma(\ell-1)}(ex)_{\ell}}\delta_{(\varepsilon^{\prime}\varsigma^{\prime})_{\tau(\ell)}(e\beta)_{\ell}}\delta_{(\varepsilon\varsigma)_{\sigma(\ell+k)}(\epsilon{y})_{\ell}}\delta_{(\varepsilon^{\prime}\varsigma^{\prime})_{\tau(\ell+k+1)}(\epsilon\theta)_{\ell}}\delta_{(\varepsilon\varsigma)_{\ell-1}(e^{\prime}{x})_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\delta_{(\varepsilon^{\prime}\varsigma^{\prime})_{\ell}(e^{\prime}\alpha)_{\ell}}\delta_{(\varepsilon\varsigma)_{\ell+k}(\epsilon^{\prime}{y})_{\ell}}\delta_{(\varepsilon^{\prime}\varsigma^{\prime})_{\ell+k+1}(\epsilon^{\prime}\lambda)_{\ell}}|\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\varsigma^{\prime}_{0}\rangle\!\langle\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(k+1)}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(k+1)}|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma,\tau\in\mathfrak{G}_{2k+2}}\hskip-10.84006pt\mathsf{Wg}(\tau\sigma^{-1})\langle\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(0)}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(0)}|\rho|\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k+1}\varsigma^{\prime}_{k+1}\rangle|\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\varsigma^{\prime}_{0}\rangle\!\langle\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(k+1)}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(k+1)}|\delta_{\varepsilon_{k}\varepsilon_{\sigma(2k+1)}}\delta_{\varepsilon_{\sigma(k)}\varepsilon_{2k+1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\delta_{\varepsilon_{\sigma(\ell-1)}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell)}}\delta_{\varepsilon_{\sigma(\ell+k)}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell+k+1)}}\delta_{\varepsilon_{\ell-1}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\ell}}\delta_{\varepsilon_{\ell+k}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\ell+k+1}}\delta_{\varsigma^{\prime}_{\ell}\alpha_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\delta_{\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell)}\beta_{\ell}}\delta_{\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell+k+1)}\theta_{\ell}}\delta_{\varsigma^{\prime}_{\ell+k+1}\lambda_{\ell}}\prod_{n=0}^{2k+1}\delta_{\varsigma_{\sigma(n)}\varsigma_{n}}.

(26)

The ancillary part is simply
\Omega_{k:0}^{(\mathsf{AB})}=d_{\mathsf{S}}^{-k}|\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}\ldots\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}\rangle\!\langle\theta_{1}\lambda_{1}\ldots\theta_{k}\lambda_{k}|
, so we get for the analogue of Eq. (
9
),

\displaystyle\Omega_{k:0}

\displaystyle=\omega_{k:0}^{(\rho)}\otimes\Omega_{k:0}^{(AB)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d_{\mathsf{S}}^{k}}\sum_{\sigma,\tau\in\mathfrak{G}_{2k+2}}\hskip-10.84006pt\mathsf{Wg}(\tau\sigma^{-1})\delta_{\varepsilon_{k}\varepsilon_{\sigma(2k+1)}}\delta_{\varepsilon_{\sigma(k)}\varepsilon_{2k+1}}

\displaystyle\qquad|\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\varsigma^{\prime}_{0}\varsigma^{\prime}_{1}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(1)}\cdots\varsigma^{\prime}_{k}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(k)}\rangle\langle\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(k+1)}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(k+1)}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(k+2)}\varsigma^{\prime}_{k+2}\cdots\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(2k+1)}\varsigma^{\prime}_{2k+1}|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\langle\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(0)}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(0)}|\rho|\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k+1}\varsigma^{\prime}_{k+1}\rangle\prod_{n=0}^{2k+1}\delta_{\varsigma_{\sigma(n)}\varsigma_{n}}\prod_{\ell=1}^{k}\delta_{\varepsilon_{\sigma(\ell-1)}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell)}}\delta_{\varepsilon_{\sigma(\ell+k)}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell+k+1)}}\delta_{\varepsilon_{\ell-1}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\ell}}\delta_{\varepsilon_{\ell+k}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\ell+k+1}},

(27)

and thus

\displaystyle\mathds{E}_{\mathsf{h}}[\tr(\Upsilon^{2})]

\displaystyle=\tr[(\rho\otimes\mathsf{\Psi}^{\otimes{k}})\,\Omega_{k:0}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2k}}\sum_{\sigma,\tau\in\mathfrak{G}_{2k+2}}\hskip-10.84006pt\mathsf{Wg}(\tau\sigma^{-1})\langle\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(0)}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(0)}|\rho|\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k+1}\varsigma^{\prime}_{k+1}\rangle\!\langle\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(k+1)}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(k+1)}|\rho|\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\varsigma^{\prime}_{0}\rangle\delta_{\varepsilon_{k}\varepsilon_{\sigma(2k+1)}}\delta_{\varepsilon_{2k+1}\varepsilon_{\sigma(k)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=1\\
\ell\neq{k+1}\end{subarray}}^{2k+1}\delta_{\varepsilon_{\ell-1}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\ell}}\delta_{\varepsilon_{\sigma(\ell-1)}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell)}}\delta_{\varsigma^{\prime}_{\ell}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell)}}\prod_{n=0}^{2k+1}\delta_{\varsigma_{\sigma(n)}\varsigma_{n}},

(28)

as stated in Eq. (
28
), where we make the definitions

\displaystyle\rho_{\tau(0);k+1}\rho_{\tau(k+1);0}=\langle\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(0)}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(0)}|\rho|\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k+1}\varsigma^{\prime}_{k+1}\rangle\!\langle\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(k+1)}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(k+1)}|\rho|\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\varsigma^{\prime}_{0}\rangle

(29)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}_{k,\sigma,\tau}^{(d_{\mathsf{E}})}=\delta_{e_{k}e_{\sigma(2k+1)}}\delta_{e_{2k+1}e_{\sigma(k)}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=1\\
\ell\neq{k+1}\end{subarray}}^{2k+1}\delta_{e_{\ell-1}e^{\prime}_{\ell}}\delta_{e_{\sigma(\ell-1)}e^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell)}},\quad\tilde{\tilde{\Delta}}_{k,\sigma,\tau}^{(d_{\mathsf{S}})}=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=1\\
\ell\neq{k+1}\end{subarray}}^{2k+1}\delta_{s^{\prime}_{\ell}{s}^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell)}}\prod_{n=0}^{2k+1}\delta_{s_{n}s_{\sigma(n)}}

\displaystyle\Delta_{k,\sigma,\tau}^{(d_{\mathsf{E}},d_{\mathsf{S}})}=\tilde{\Delta}_{k,\sigma,\tau}^{(d_{\mathsf{E}})}\tilde{\tilde{\Delta}}_{k,\sigma,\tau}^{(d_{\mathsf{S}})}

(30)

1
Small subsystem limit

In the
d_{\mathsf{E}}\to\infty
limit the only term that remains is the one with

\displaystyle\sigma=\tau=(0,k+1)(1,k+2)\cdots(k,2k+1),

(31)

as expressed in cycle notation, which simply means
\sigma(0)=k+1=\tau(0)
,
\sigma(k+1)=0=\tau(k+1)
for the first
(0,k+1)
, and similarly for the rest of assignments. This then leads to the correct limit, as

\displaystyle\tilde{\tilde{\Delta}}_{k,\sigma,\tau}^{(d_{\mathsf{S}})}\to\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varsigma^{(\prime)}_{i}=1\\
\varsigma^{\prime}\neq\{\varsigma^{\prime}_{0},\varsigma^{\prime}_{k+1}\}\end{subarray}}^{d_{\mathsf{S}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=1\\
\ell\neq{k+1}\end{subarray}}^{2k+1}\delta_{\varsigma^{\prime}_{\ell}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell)}}\prod_{n=0}^{2k+1}\delta_{\varsigma_{\sigma(n)}\varsigma_{n}}

\displaystyle={d_{\mathsf{S}}}^{2k+1},

(32)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}_{k,\sigma,\tau}^{(d_{\mathsf{E}})}\to\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varepsilon^{(\prime)}_{i}=1\\
\varepsilon^{\prime}\neq\{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k+1}\}\end{subarray}}^{d_{\mathsf{E}}}\delta_{\varepsilon_{k}\varepsilon_{\sigma(2k+1)}}\delta_{\varepsilon_{2k+1}\varepsilon_{\sigma(k)}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=1\\
\ell\neq{k+1}\end{subarray}}^{2k+1}\delta_{\varepsilon_{\ell-1}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\ell}}\delta_{\varepsilon_{\sigma(\ell-1)}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\tau(\ell)}}

\displaystyle={d_{\mathsf{E}}}^{2k+2},

(33)

while keeping
\sigma\tau^{-1}=\mathbf{1}^{2k+2}
, i.e. with the argument in the
\mathsf{Wg}
function an identity (or fixed point), which generates the least denominator powers because
\#\boldsymbol{1}^{n}=\#[(1)(2)\cdots(n)]=n
, i.e. the identity generates the most number of cycles. When considering other permutations for
\sigma
and
\tau
we see that the
d_{\mathsf{E}}
powers in the numerator can only decrease while those in the denominator can only increase (as the identity is the permutation that generates the most cycles) so indeed in the large subsystem limit this is the only pair of permutations that survive; the terms in
\rho
yield traces when summed over basis vectors and thus give factors of one,

\displaystyle\sum_{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k+1},\varsigma^{\prime}_{k+1}}\langle\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k+1}\varsigma^{\prime}_{k+1}|\rho|\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k+1}\varsigma^{\prime}_{k+1}\rangle=\sum_{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0},\varsigma^{\prime}_{0}}\langle\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\varsigma^{\prime}_{0}|\rho|\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\varsigma^{\prime}_{0}\rangle=\tr\rho=1.

(34)

This leads then to the conclusion

\displaystyle\mathds{E}_{\mathsf{h}}[\tr(\Upsilon^{2})]

\displaystyle\sim\frac{d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2k+1}d_{\mathsf{E}}^{2k+2}}{d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2k}}\mathsf{Wg}(\mathbf{1}^{2k+2})=d_{\mathsf{S}}d_{\mathsf{E}}^{2k+2}\frac{1}{(d_{\mathsf{E}}d_{\mathsf{S}})^{2k+2}}=\frac{1}{d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2k+1}},\hskip 7.22743pt\text{when}\,\,d_{\mathsf{E}}\to\infty.

(35)

2
Long time limit

For the case when
k\to\infty
, the
\mathsf{Wg}
function behaves dominantly as in the small subsystem limit and the only pair of permutations that identify all dimension powers in the numerator without dependence of
k
are identities
\sigma=\tau=\mathbf{1}^{2k+2}
. In this case

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varsigma^{(\prime)}_{i}=1\\
\varsigma^{\prime}\neq\{\varsigma^{\prime}_{0},\varsigma^{\prime}_{k+1}\}\end{subarray}}^{d_{\mathsf{S}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=1\\
\ell\neq{k+1}\end{subarray}}^{2k+1}\delta_{\varsigma^{\prime}_{\ell}\varsigma^{\prime}_{\ell}}\prod_{n=0}^{2k+1}\delta_{\varsigma_{n}\varsigma_{n}}

\displaystyle={d_{\mathsf{S}}}^{4k+2},

(36)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varepsilon^{(\prime)}_{i}=1\\
\varepsilon^{\prime}\neq\{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k+1}\}\end{subarray}}^{d_{\mathsf{E}}}\delta_{\varepsilon_{k}\varepsilon_{2k+1}}\delta_{\varepsilon_{2k+1}\varepsilon_{k}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=1\\
\ell\neq{k+1}\end{subarray}}^{2k+1}\delta_{\varepsilon_{\ell-1}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\ell}}\delta_{\varepsilon_{\ell-1}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\ell}}

\displaystyle={d_{\mathsf{E}}}^{2k+1},

(37)

giving

\displaystyle\mathds{E}_{\mathsf{h}}[\tr(\Upsilon^{2})]

\displaystyle\sim\frac{d_{\mathsf{S}}^{4k+2}d_{\mathsf{E}}^{2k+1}}{d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2k}}\tr(\rho^{2})\mathsf{Wg}(\mathbf{1}^{2k+2})=\frac{d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2k+2}d_{\mathsf{E}}^{2k+1}}{(d_{\mathsf{E}}d_{\mathsf{S}})^{2k+2}}=\frac{1}{d_{\mathsf{E}}},\hskip 7.22743pt\text{when}\,\,k\to\infty,

(38)

as the fiducial state
\rho
is pure,
\tr(\rho^{2})=1
, by assumption.

Chapter 9
Sampling from the unitary group

Here we present detail for how the numerical sampling and therefore the plot in Fig.
3
of Chapter
4
was obtained. We follow Ref.
[
66
]
, which contains a complete discussion and reasoning behind the algorithm to numerically sample unitaries from the Haar measure.

Specifically, we computed the bound on the non-Markovianity
\mathcal{N}_{1}
given by the trace distance
D
between a process
\Upsilon
sampled at random and the maximally noisy process
\mathds{1}/d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2k+1}
, i.e.
D\left(\Upsilon,\mathds{1}/d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2k+1}\right)\geq\mathcal{N}_{1}
. This amounts to sampling unitaries
U_{i}\sim\mu_{\mathsf{h}}
of dimension
d_{\mathsf{ES}}\times{d}_{\mathsf{ES}}
, with which the full process is defined, and then computing its trace distance with respect to the maximally mixed state of dimension
d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2k+1}
. This is repeated a given number of times
n
in order to obtain an arithmetic average,
\mathrm{E}_{n}
, which we compare with the upper-bound
\mathcal{B}_{k}\geq\mathds{E}_{\mathfrak{h}}[\mathcal{N}_{1}]
. These must satisfy
\mathrm{E}_{n}\leq\mathcal{B}_{k}
, given that
\mathcal{B}_{k}
is in turn an upper bound on the uniform (Haar) average trace distance between a process and the maximally noisy process.

The algorithm in Ref.
[
66
]
to obtain a uniformly distributed
d\times{d}
unitary matrix is as follows, with each step explained below:

i.

Generate a
d\times{d}
complex matrix
Z
with independent identically distributed (i.i.d.) standard normal complex random variables.

ii.

Perform a
QR
decomposition of
Z
.

iii.

Define a diagonal matrix
\Lambda=\mathrm{diag}\left(\frac{r_{1}}{|r_{1}|},\cdots,\frac{r_{d}}{|r_{d}|}\right)
, where
r_{i}
are the diagonal elements of
R
.

iv.

The matrix
U=Q\Lambda
is distributed according to the Haar measure.

The first step is straightforward, where here “
standard normal complex
” refers to a complex random variable with real and imaginary parts being independent normally distributed random variables with mean zero and variance
1/2
. This means the components
Z_{ij}
are normally distributed with a
PDF
given by
\mathscr{P}(Z_{ij})=\frac{1}{\pi}\exp\left(-|Z_{ij}|^{2}\right)
. Since these components are statistically independent, the joint distribution for
Z
is simply the product of distributions so that
\mathscr{P}(Z)=\frac{1}{\pi^{d^{2}}}\exp\left[-\tr(ZZ^{\dagger})\right]
. This distribution describes the so-called
Ginibre ensemble
, and the variable
Z
induces a probability measure
d\mu_{\mathsf{G}}
on
\mathrm{GL}(\mathds{C},d)
, the set of
d\times{d}
invertible matrices with ordinary matrix multiplication. Importantly, this measure on the Ginibre ensemble is left and right invariant under unitary transformations,
d\mu_{\mathsf{G}}(UZ)=d\mu_{\mathsf{G}}(ZV)=d\mu_{\mathsf{G}}(Z)
for any fixed
U,V\in\mathds{U}(d)
.

For step two, any matrix
Z\in\mathrm{GL}(\mathds{C},d)
can be decomposed as
Z=QR
with
Q\in\mathds{U}(d)
and
R
an upper-triangular and invertible matrix
[
48
]
. This is called the
QR
decomposition of
Z
. This means that, because the matrix
R
is invertible, we can write
Q=ZR^{-1}
and we get a unitary random matrix. The
QR
decomposition is a standard routine in most symbolic softwares such as Matlab or Mathematica or in packages like NumPy for Python.

The last step is related to the non-uniqueness of the
QR
decomposition. As discussed in Ref.
[
66
]
, the unitary
Q
is not quite Haar-distributed because of this non-uniqueness. That is, for any diagonal unitary matrix
\Lambda\in\mathds{U}(d)
, we have
QR=(Q\Lambda)(\Lambda^{\dagger}{R})=Q^{\prime}R^{\prime}
with
Q^{\prime}
unitary and
R^{\prime}
upper-triangular. This is remedied with
\Lambda=\mathrm{diag}\left(\frac{r_{1}}{|r_{1}|},\cdots,\frac{r_{d}}{|r_{d}|}\right)
because it forces
R
to have positive diagonal entries.

Thus, for a
k
-step random interaction process, i.e. with
U_{i}\neq{U}_{j}
, in the
i\textsuperscript{th}
run, we generate
k+1
Haar-distributed
d_{\mathsf{SE}}\times{d}_{\mathsf{SE}}
unitary matrices,
\left\{U^{(i)}_{0},U^{(i)}_{1},\ldots,U^{(i)}_{k}\right\}
, according to the algorithm above and compute the arithmetic average

\mathrm{E}_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D\left(\Upsilon^{(i)},\mathds{1}/d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2k+1}\right),

(1)

for a given number
n
of trials, where
\Upsilon^{(i)}=\tr_{\mathsf{E}}\left[\mathcal{U}_{k}^{(i)}\mathcal{S}_{k}\cdots\mathcal{U}_{1}^{(i)}\mathcal{S}_{1}\mathcal{U}_{0}^{(i)}(\rho\otimes\mathsf{\Psi})\right]
with
\mathcal{U}_{\ell}^{(i)}(\cdot)=U_{\ell}^{(i)}(\cdot)U_{\ell}^{(i)\,\dagger}
, is the random process in the
i\textsuperscript{th}
trial for any fiducial pure state
\rho
.

Finally, the greatest problem to be overcome is the large-dimensional nature of the Choi state, which prior to partial tracing of the environment, is a
d_{\mathsf{E}}d_{\mathsf{S}}^{2k+1}
dimensional square matrix. While we directly computed Eq. (
1
), depending on the purpose of the calculation,it might be worth it to try and render this problem efficient through other computational or numerical techniques.
